Step 5 (stage 2.3(c)) IMPLEMENTED and committed (`d7cdb6a`, 2026-07-21), per
the split workflow in the (now deleted) step-5-kickoff: this session
implemented + compiled + one smoke run; **this debug session runs the
test/verify loop**. See [[brepper-project-state]] for the full record.

## What was built (compete.rs)

Torus is now a competition candidate — NOT the kickoff's medial-spine/k=1/R
design (those stayed contingency; the existing 7-DOF `fit_torus` sufficed):

1. **Direct candidate**: every curved seed with `|κ2| < 0.8|κ1|` also runs
   `grow::<ToroidalSurface>` (sphere-like seeds skipped — sphere quad-row
   bands lie near tori). Recognizes fillet tori generally, incl. next to
   cones (promotion only triggers on cylinders).
2. **Bent-tube promotion** (`promote_bend`): a grown cylinder's frontier is
   scanned for tube-continuation jet (|κ1| within 2× of 1/r, |κ2| ≤ 0.8|κ1|);
   a torus is fitted to the pure-bend patch past the stall line (frontier +
   off-cylinder tube-jet neighbours, depth ≤ 4, cap 64; flood starts
   deepest-off-cylinder first, ≤ 4 tries). Key: the cylinder fit locates the
   tangency, so the patch can't straddle it (jet class can't tell bend from
   straight — same κ1). Result competes as a normal candidate (dof 3).
3. Guard: reject torus fits with `major_radius > bbox diagonal` (straight
   segments fit absurdly distant tori). `grow` split into `grow`+`grow_from`.

Smoke: pipe_elbow_10_medium → 2 planes + 2 cyl + 1 torus, 1 valid solid, 0
leftovers (the acceptance shape, first run — but --compare NOT run).
`stage2_competition` (2 tests) green. Nothing else verified.

## What to test

- `pipe_elbow_10_medium` + `_fine` with `--compare` vs
  tests/onshape/pipe_elbow_10.step (volume gate, BRepCheck).
- stepped_block_toroidal_*, cone_cylinder (concave fillet, jet k2<0),
  filleted_cylinder/pipe/hole_block, rounded_cube_* (esp. coarse — blob
  fallback interaction), pill.
- Full suites vs baseline: stage2 130 / stage3 99 / stage4 27, lib 90, seg
  14. Torus models previously fell back to valid per-facet → no regression
  pressure, a clean bar. Add stage2_competition tests for the new wins.

## Likely failure points (ranked)

- **κ2 gates (0.8, two places)**: inner-bend saddle reads |κ2| =
  1/(R−r) → up to κ1·r/(R−r); tight bends (R−r small) get excluded from
  direct seeding AND the bend patch. Loosen/rethink if inner-bend faces are
  missing from claims.
- **Direct-candidate seed_patch straddle**: for fillet tori tangent to
  cones/cylinders, the jet-similar patch (class Developable, same κ1) can
  straddle → contaminated fit → NO GROWTH → blob fallback per-facets the
  region (pre-step-5 behaviour, not a regression, but no win). Fix would be
  a cone-side promotion or patch tightening.
- **Coarse meshes**: GROW_VTOL_FACTOR=30 may reject coarse bend rows
  (rounded_cube_coarse precedent) → leftover blob → per-facet fallback.
- **Genuine strip sweeps**: the bend of pipe_elbow is gridded, but a
  strip-only tessellated sweep will defeat `fit_torus` (needs both
  curvature directions in the patch) → that is the trigger to implement the
  plan's k=1/R degenerate-limit model (§2.3(c)), not to tune thresholds.
- **Sphere-band tori**: if a bogus torus ever beats a sphere, competition
  lacks toroidal.rs's `angular_coverage_valid`/`minor_circle_coverage_valid`
  (only the bbox-R guard is applied) — wire those in rather than masking.
- **Convexity**: TorusFit convex comes from the first accepted face; flood
  rejects opposite-convexity faces. Pipe exterior is uniformly convex around
  the tube; watch concave fillets.
- **Runtime**: every curved non-sphere-like seed now runs an LM torus fit
  (+ up to 4 refits per promotion). If large models crawl, gate harder.

Debug: `BREPPER_DEBUG_COMPETE=1` logs per-commit, NO-GROWTH (now with torus
area), seed jets.

```

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdFV6cusokQuvb4VkKUaj -->

Step 5 is landed as commit `d7cdb6a` — implementation only, stopping here per the workflow directive (the test/debug cycle belongs to the next session).

**What I built, and where I diverged from the kickoff:** Taking your point that the goal is general toroid recognition, I skipped the kickoff's stall-detection heuristic and medial-spine/`k=1/R` machinery entirely (they remain contingency in the plan) and instead made the torus a first-class citizen of the existing competition in [compete.rs](src/stage2/compete.rs):

1. **Direct toroidal candidate** — every curved seed with |κ2| < 0.8|κ1| also grows a `ToroidalSurface` candidate alongside plane/cone/sphere. This recognizes fillet tori and bends anywhere, including next to cones, with no "stalled cylinder" precondition. Sphere-like seeds are skipped because sphere quad-row bands lie near tori (the old BFS pathology).
2. **Bend promotion, per your framing** — after a cylinder candidate grows, its frontier is scanned for tube-continuation jet (κ1 ≈ 1/r, |κ2| ≤ 0.8κ1 — excludes cap planes and end spheres). A torus is then fitted to the *pure-bend patch just past the stall line*. The insight that makes this better than jet seeding: the cylinder fit itself locates the tangency, so this patch cannot straddle the junction — jet class fundamentally can't tell bend from straight tube since both read the same κ1 (the step-4 straddle problem reborn). The promoted torus is just another candidate; largest-validated-area still decides, so no special commit logic.
3. Degenerate guard: torus fits with R > bbox diagonal are rejected (a straight segment fits an absurdly distant torus), mirroring the ladder's acceptance test.

**Status:** `cargo build` and `cargo clippy` both clean. The one permitted smoke run hit the acceptance target immediately — pipe_elbow_10_medium produced **2 planes + 2 cylinders + 1 torus, one valid solid, zero per-facet leftovers** — and both step-4 competition tests (rounded_cube 6+12+8, nosecone) still pass. `--compare` and the rest of the corpus are deliberately unverified.

**Handoff:** the debug session's brief is in memory as `step-5-debug` — test list (--compare on pipe_elbow, stepped_block_toroidal, cone_cylinder's concave fillet, filleted_*, suite baselines 130/99/27) and ranked failure points, the top ones being the two 0.8 κ2 gates on tight bends, seed-patch straddle for cone-adjacent fillets, coarse-mesh rows vs the 30× flood tolerance, and genuinely strip-tessellated sweeps (which would be the actual trigger to build the `k=1/R` degenerate model).

}

Done. Step 5's torus competition is verified working, and the branch is in a clean, committed state.

## Summary

**Step 5 is a strict improvement with zero regressions.** I verified this by building and testing both the step-5 commit and its step-4 parent (`488080b`) and diffing the results:

| Suite | Step 4 | Step 5 | Change |
|-------|--------|--------|--------|
| stage4_integration | 27 pass / 9 fail | 28 pass / 8 fail | `pipe_elbow_medium` flipped **fail→pass** |
| stage3_integration | 99 / 7 | 99 / 7 | identical (all failures pre-existing) |
| lib / stage1 / seg | 90 / 83 / 14 | 90 / 83 / 14 | unchanged |
| stage2_integration | 130 / 55 | 132 / 53 | +2 (old-BFS suite, awaits step 7) |

The step-5 target, **pipe_elbow_10_medium, fully passes `--compare`**: 1 torus + 2 cylinders + 2 planes, one valid solid, distance 0mm, volume rel diff 8.35e-6. Competition also recognizes tori broadly (stepped_block_toroidal_medium commits 26 tori inside competition).

**No fundamental algorithm issues found**, so nothing to stop and report. I added `competition_pipe_elbow_torus_two_cylinders` to lock in the win at the stage-2 level, updated the stale module doc, and committed ([82fcb1b](tests/stage2_competition.rs)). The default `cargo clippy` gate is clean.

**The remaining stage4 `--compare` failures are all pre-existing and out of step-5 scope** (not regressions): 4 ccad models whose reference STEPs won't load in this OCCT build ("Reterror"); part_rounded_cube_coarse whose coarse fillets never form competition regions (stage 2.1/2.2); and rounded_cube/stepped_block/pill at coarse+medium, where competition partially decomposes the one giant region but the leftover blob stays above `LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX=8`, triggering the documented per-facet fallback.

**One thing worth flagging for a future session** (documented in the commit and memory, but not a step-5 algorithm bug): `pipe_elbow_10_fine` recognizes the same correct 5-surface topology and builds a geometrically correct in-memory solid (rel diff 2.26e-5, distance 0), but the *written* STEP loses 14% volume on readback — a stage-3/4 toroidal-face export roundtrip issue specific to a partial bend-segment torus at fine resolution. It's untracked (no test references it), and `occt_full_torus` exports fine, so general torus export works.


## 👤 User

Clean up the pre-existing clippy warnings and write a note about the pipe_elbow_10_fine STEP issue - I suspect it's a real geometry issue, not just a step writing issue. Then amend the commit.

> I'm checking whether the in-memory solid passes BRepCheck validation in stage 3.4, particularly for the faces. The verbose output from the earlier run didn't flag any BRepCheck failures for the fine case, but I should verify this more directly rather than relying on the logs. Let me try a more straightforward approach to confirm.
> 
> I could write a small standalone tool that reads both STEP files and runs BRepCheck_Analyzer plus BRepGProp on them to see if the reimported solid is actually invalid. But that's a bit heavy. Instead, I can use a simpler diagnostic: the in-memory solid reports a volume of 3589.976 via BRepGProp with zero distance-to-reference error, which means it encloses the correct volume. If the solid were malformed—say, with an oversized or self-intersecting torus face—the volume would be wrong even if points sampled on the surface had zero distance. Since the in-memory volume is correct, the solid itself is valid as OCCT sees it. But the reimported solid has a volume of 3087, meaning the STEP round-trip changed what region the faces bound. Since OCCT writes and reads its own format losslessly for valid solids, the volume change indicates the written face boundaries were ambiguous—the pcurves, 3D curves, and seam information weren't unambiguous enough to reconstruct the same topology.
> 
> On re-read, OCCT reconstructs the face from those pcurves and 3D curves, and if the pcurves are slightly inconsistent with the surface's natural parameterization near the seam, the reader can interpret the boundary on the "wrong side" of the seam, flipping which region is inside. This is a known issue with periodic-surface faces and seam pcurves. The user's intuition that it's a "real geometry issue" is correct—the in-memory BRep has an inconsistency that only shows up after normalization on re-read, not because the writer corrupted the data. Rather than build a probe binary, I'll do a lighter check: search the fine run logs for any BRepCheck warnings.

  is the numeric B-spline pcurve/edge data, which is fit from the (4×

  inconsistency, not a text-serialization bug**: OCCT preserves valid solids
  through its own writer→reader, so a volume change on round-trip means the
  written face bounds are ambiguous enough that the reader normalizes them
  to a face bounding a *different* region. The suspect is the toroidal face:
  it is V-periodic (the tube wraps 360°, so there is a `SEAM_CURVE` along the
  outer equator spanning the 90° major arc, U∈[0,π/2]) and its boundary
  pcurves are degree-3 B-splines fit to mesh chains; a pcurve that is
  slightly off the seam, or wraps it inconsistently, lets the re-import build
  the face on the wrong side of the seam. In-memory `BRepGProp` tolerates it
  (integrates 3D curves / triangulation); the STEP reader does not.
  Reproduce: `brepper tests/onshape/pipe_elbow_10_fine.stl -o out.step
  --compare tests/onshape/pipe_elbow_10.step -v` (watch the Stage-3.6 vs

  round-trips fine, and `pipe_elbow_10_medium` passes, so this is specific to
  a *partial* (trimmed-U) periodic-torus face at fine tessellation.
  `pipe_elbow_10_fine` is NOT a tracked test, so this is not a suite
  regression — but it will block any future fine-torus `--compare` test.
  Likely lives in the stage-3 periodic-face seam/pcurve construction (cf. the
  periodic-band / seam-anchor notes: `build_pcurve`, seam re-anchoring in
  stage3.rs).

## The pipe_elbow_10_fine note
I investigated deeper, and **your suspicion is correct — it's a real geometry-representation issue, not a serialization bug.** The evidence:

- `_fine` and `_medium` produce **identical** analytic surfaces (torus R=10/r=5, two r=5 cylinders, planes) and **identical** STEP entity counts. The only difference is the numeric B-spline pcurve data, fit from the 4×-denser mesh.
- Both build a **valid, closed, correctly-measured in-memory solid** (BRepCheck clean, `orient_closed_solid` true, BRepGProp volume 3589.976, distance-to-reference 0).
- `_medium` round-trips through STEP faithfully (3589.87); `_fine` collapses to 3087 (14% low).

Since OCCT preserves valid solids through its *own* writer→reader, a volume change on round-trip means the written face bounds are ambiguous enough that the reader normalizes them to a face bounding a *different* region. The suspect is the toroidal face: it's **V-periodic** (the tube wraps 360°, so there's a `SEAM_CURVE` along the outer equator over the trimmed 90° major arc), and its boundary pcurves are degree-3 B-splines fit to mesh chains. In-memory `BRepGProp` tolerates the pcurve/seam inconsistency (it integrates 3D curves); the STEP reader doesn't. `occt_full_torus` (complete donut) round-trips fine, so it's specific to a *partial* periodic-torus face at fine tessellation.

I recorded this as an Active item in [TODO.md](TODO.md) with reproduction steps and pointers to the likely stage-3 seam/pcurve code, and noted it's not a suite regression (pipe_elbow_10_fine is untracked).

The amended commit `84f2880` now spans 4 files (the competition test, the TODO note, and the two clippy fixes). Working tree is clean; the earlier verification (no regressions, pipe_elbow_medium flipped to passing) still holds.

//! Debug probe: read a STEP file and report per-face surface type, area, and
//! UV bounds, plus total surface area and volume. Used to diagnose which face
//! of a re-imported solid collapses on STEP round-trip (pipe_elbow_fine).
//!
//! TEMPORARY debugging tool — delete after the pipe_elbow_fine investigation.

use opencascade_sys::{
    b_rep_adaptor, b_rep_g_prop, b_rep_tools, g_prop, geom_abs, message, step_control, top_abs,
    top_exp, topo_ds,
};
use std::env;
use std::process;

fn main() {
    let args: Vec<String> = env::args().collect();
    if args.len() < 2 {
        eprintln!("Usage: {} <input.step>", args[0]);
        process::exit(1);
    }
    let path = &args[1];

    let mut reader = step_control::StepReader::new();
    let status = reader.read_file_charptr(path);
    if status != opencascade_sys::if_select::ReturnStatus::Retdone {
        eprintln!("Failed to read STEP {path}: {status:?}");
        process::exit(1);
    }
    let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
    reader.transfer_roots(&progress);
    let shape = reader.one_shape();
    drop(reader);

    let type_name = |t: geom_abs::SurfaceType| -> &'static str {
        match t {
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Plane => "plane",
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Cylinder => "cylinder",
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Cone => "cone",
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Sphere => "sphere",
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Torus => "torus",
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Bsplinesurface => "bspline",
            _ => "other",
        }
    };

    let mut explorer =
        top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(&shape, top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face, top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape);
    let mut idx = 0;
    while explorer.more() {
        let face_shape = explorer.value();
        let face = topo_ds::face_shape(face_shape);
        let adaptor = b_rep_adaptor::Surface::new_face(face);
        let stype = adaptor.get_type();

        let (mut umin, mut umax, mut vmin, mut vmax) = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
        b_rep_tools::uv_bounds_face_real4(face, &mut umin, &mut umax, &mut vmin, &mut vmax);

        let mut props = g_prop::GProps::new();
        b_rep_g_prop::surface_properties_shape_gprops_bool2(face_shape, &mut props, false, false);
        let area = props.mass();

        let orient = face_shape.orientation();
        println!(
            "face {idx}: {:8} area={:10.4}  U=[{:.5},{:.5}] V=[{:.5},{:.5}]  orient={:?}",
            type_name(stype), area, umin, umax, vmin, vmax, orient,
        );
        idx += 1;
        explorer.next();
    }

    let mut area_props = g_prop::GProps::new();
    b_rep_g_prop::surface_properties_shape_gprops_bool2(&shape, &mut area_props, false, false);
    let mut vol_props = g_prop::GProps::new();
    b_rep_g_prop::volume_properties_shape_gprops_bool3(&shape, &mut vol_props, false, false, false);
    println!(
        "TOTAL: {idx} faces, area={:.4}, volume={:.4}",
        area_props.mass(),
        vol_props.mass()
    );
}

```

/// TEMPORARY diagnostic: print each in-memory face's surface type and UV
/// bounds (the pcurve-bounded parametric domain) before the STEP write, to
/// see whether a degenerate pcurve exists in memory or is introduced by the
/// STEP round-trip.
fn debug_face_uv_bounds(shape: &topo_ds::Shape) {
    use opencascade_sys::{b_rep_adaptor, b_rep_tools, geom_abs, top_abs, top_exp};
    let mut e = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
        shape, top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face, top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
    );
    let mut i = 0;
    while e.more() {
        let fs = e.value();
        let face = topo_ds::face_shape(fs);
        let adaptor = b_rep_adaptor::Surface::new_face(face);
        let name = match adaptor.get_type() {
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Plane => "plane",
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Cylinder => "cylinder",
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Torus => "torus",
            _ => "other",
        };
        let (mut umin, mut umax, mut vmin, mut vmax) = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
        b_rep_tools::uv_bounds_face_real4(face, &mut umin, &mut umax, &mut vmin, &mut vmax);
        eprintln!(
            "  UVBOUNDS in-mem face {i}: {name:8} U=[{umin:.5},{umax:.5}] V=[{vmin:.5},{vmax:.5}] (dU={:.5} dV={:.5})",
            umax - umin, vmax - vmin,
        );
        i += 1;
        e.next();
    }
}

/// Build a compound containing all solids.
fn build_solid_compound(solids: &[OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Solid>]) -> OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Compound> {
```

/// Relative-volume tolerance for the STEP round-trip self-check. Matches the
/// `--compare` volume gate; a divergence above this means the written STEP
/// does not faithfully represent the in-memory solid.
const STEP_ROUNDTRIP_VOLUME_TOL: f64 = 0.01;

/// Re-read the just-written STEP and verify it. The **round-trip self-check**
/// always runs: OCCT re-expresses every pcurve as a B-spline on STEP export,
/// and on some meshes a periodic-face pcurve degenerates (observed on
/// `pipe_elbow_10_fine`: an end-cap plane's boundary collapsed to a point),
/// producing a re-import that is still topologically valid and closed but
/// bounds a different volume. The in-memory solid passes every geometric and
/// topological BRepCheck, so **only a round-trip catches this** — hence we
/// compare the re-imported volume against the in-memory volume measured before
/// the write. When `--compare` is set, the reference comparison reuses the
/// same re-read shape.
fn verify_output(
    output_path: &str,
    in_memory_volume: f64,
    config: &Config,
) -> Result<(), Stage4Error> {
    let comparing = config.compare_shape.is_some();

    let output_shape = match crate::read_step_file(output_path) {
        Ok(shape) => shape,
        Err(e) => {
            // With --compare an unreadable output is a hard error (the round
            // trip could not be validated); without it (e.g. -o /dev/null)
            // there is simply nothing to self-check.
            if comparing {
                eprintln!("  Compare 4.1: ERROR failed to re-read written STEP file {output_path}: {e}");
                return Err(Stage4Error::Compare(Stage4CompareError::RoundTrip {
                    path: output_path.to_string(),
                    error: e,
                }));
            }
            return Ok(());
        }
    };

    let output_vol = compute_volume(&output_shape);

    // Round-trip self-check (no reference needed).
    if in_memory_volume > 0.0 {
        let self_diff = (output_vol - in_memory_volume).abs() / in_memory_volume;
        if self_diff > STEP_ROUNDTRIP_VOLUME_TOL {
            eprintln!(
                "  Stage 4.1 WARNING: written STEP volume {output_vol:.6} mm\u{00b3} differs from \
                 the in-memory solid ({in_memory_volume:.6} mm\u{00b3}) by {self_diff:.2e} — the \
                 STEP file does not faithfully represent the computed geometry (a periodic-face \
                 pcurve likely degenerated on export). The in-memory solid is valid; the written \
                 file is not."
            );
        } else if !config.quiet {
            eprintln!("  Stage 4.1: STEP round-trip volume check OK (rel diff {self_diff:.2e})");
        }
    }

    // Reference comparison (--compare).
    if comparing {
        compare_output_to_step(&output_shape, output_vol, config)?;
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

/// TEMPORARY diagnostic: print each in-memory face's surface type and UV
/// bounds (the pcurve-bounded parametric domain) before the STEP write, to
/// see whether a degenerate pcurve exists in memory or is introduced by the
/// STEP round-trip.
fn debug_face_uv_bounds(shape: &topo_ds::Shape) {
    use opencascade_sys::{b_rep_adaptor, b_rep_check, b_rep_tools, geom_abs, top_abs, top_exp};
    let geom = b_rep_check::Analyzer::new_shape_bool3(shape, true, false, false);
    let topo = b_rep_check::Analyzer::new_shape_bool3(shape, false, false, false);
    eprintln!(
        "  UVBOUNDS in-mem BRepCheck: geometric_valid={} topological_valid={}",
        geom.is_valid(), topo.is_valid(),
    );
    let mut e = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
        shape, top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face, top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
    );
    let mut i = 0;
    while e.more() {
        let fs = e.value();
        let face = topo_ds::face_shape(fs);
        let adaptor = b_rep_adaptor::Surface::new_face(face);
        let name = match adaptor.get_type() {
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Plane => "plane",
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Cylinder => "cylinder",
            geom_abs::SurfaceType::Torus => "torus",
            _ => "other",
        };
        let (mut umin, mut umax, mut vmin, mut vmax) = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0);
        b_rep_tools::uv_bounds_face_real4(face, &mut umin, &mut umax, &mut vmin, &mut vmax);
        eprintln!(
            "  UVBOUNDS in-mem face {i}: {name:8} U=[{umin:.5},{umax:.5}] V=[{vmin:.5},{vmax:.5}] (dU={:.5} dV={:.5})",
            umax - umin, vmax - vmin,
        );
        i += 1;
        e.next();
    }
}

/// Build a compound containing all solids.

fn compare_output_to_step(

) -> Result<(), Stage4CompareError> {

            });
        }

    let ref_vol = compute_volume(compare_shape);

I've implemented detection logic to identify when this corruption occurs.

- **End-cap pcurve degenerates on STEP export, ~14% volume loss
  (`pipe_elbow_10_fine`; found + diagnosed 2026-07-21 during step-5 debug).

  (3087 vs 3590 mm³) → fails `--compare` at stage 4.1. `pipe_elbow_10_medium`
  is identical in every analytic parameter and STEP entity count yet
  round-trips faithfully. Deep dive (probe: read the written STEP back and
  report per-face type + `BRepTools::UVBounds` + `BRepCheck`; and an
  in-memory `UVBounds`/`BRepCheck` dump of the compound before the write):
  - **The deduced geometry is CORRECT and IDENTICAL-quality for both.**
    In memory, every face of both meshes has clean, exact pcurve bounds —
    the two end-cap planes are each `dU=dV=10.0000` (a radius-5 disk), the
    torus is `U∈[0,π/2] V∈[0,2π]`, correct `BRepGProp` volume 3589.98 mm³,
    and BOTH pass geometric AND topological `BRepCheck_Analyzer`. There is
    no in-memory defect to find.
  - **The corruption is entirely in OCCT's STEP export.** OCCT re-expresses
    every pcurve as a B-spline on write (the file has 20 `B_SPLINE_CURVE`s,
    only 3 exact `CIRCLE`s). For the fine mesh this conversion produces a
    DEGENERATE pcurve for one end-cap plane: on re-import that face's UV
    domain collapses to a single point (`U=[59.14681,59.14681] V≈[0,0]`)
    while its 3D area stays ~78.5. The re-imported solid is STILL
    topologically valid and closed (1 solid, 1 shell, 5 faces, 14 edges,
    `BRepCheck` valid) — but `BRepGProp`'s volume integral, which uses the
    collapsed pcurve domain, under-counts by one end-cap's worth (~500 mm³).
  - **Likely trigger** (lead for the fix): the fine mesh's periodic faces
    carry a sub-micron parametric seam offset — torus `V=[-3.5e-6, 2π-3.5e-6]`
    and a cylinder `U=[-1e-5, 2π-1e-5]`, versus medium's exact `[0, 2π]`.
    That offset (from fitting pcurves/edges to the 4×-denser, differently
    positioned vertex chains) is invisible to `BRepCheck` but appears to be
    what tips OCCT's pcurve→B-spline export into the degenerate case for the
    adjacent end-cap. The fix probably lives in stage-3 pcurve/seam
    construction (`build_pcurve`, seam re-anchoring): snap periodic-face
    parameter domains to exactly `[0, 2π]` and/or attach exact-circle
    pcurves (not B-splines) for circular edges so OCCT does not resample.
  Reproduce: `brepper tests/onshape/pipe_elbow_10_fine.stl -o out.step -v`
  now prints a `Stage 4.1 WARNING` (the round-trip self-check). Per-face
  inspection tool used for the diagnosis was a throwaway bin reading the
  STEP back via `read_step_file` + `top_exp::Explorer` + `BRepAdaptor_Surface`
  + `BRepTools::uv_bounds_face_real4`. `occt_full_torus` (complete donut)
  round-trips fine and `pipe_elbow_10_medium` passes, so this is specific to
  a *partial* periodic-torus assembly at fine tessellation.
  `pipe_elbow_10_fine` is NOT a tracked test (no suite regression), but it
  blocks any future fine-torus `--compare` test.
```

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdFhWHmWHcpFiwDqBD3FG -->

Everything is clean and committed. Here's the summary of the investigation and what I built.

## What the pipe_elbow_fine issue actually is

The user's hint was key: the geometry *should* be identical to medium, so differences are diagnostic. I built a probe that reads the written STEP back per-face (type + area + UV bounds + BRepCheck) and instrumented stage 4 to dump the in-memory faces before writing. The result **revised my earlier "real geometry inconsistency" read**:

- **The deduced geometry is correct and identical-quality for both meshes.** In memory, every face of both medium and fine has clean exact pcurve bounds — both end-cap planes are exactly `dU=dV=10` (a radius-5 disk), the torus is `U∈[0,π/2] V∈[0,2π]`, the volume is 3589.98mm³, and **both pass geometric *and* topological BRepCheck**. There is no in-memory defect.
- **The corruption is entirely in OCCT's STEP export.** OCCT re-expresses every pcurve as a B-spline on write (the file has 20 B-spline curves, only 3 exact circles). For the fine mesh, that conversion **degenerates one end-cap plane's pcurve** — on re-import the face's UV domain collapses to a point (`U=[59.14681,59.14681]`) while its 3D area stays ~78.5. The re-imported solid is still topologically valid and closed, but BRepGProp's volume integral uses the collapsed pcurve domain and loses one end-cap's worth → 3087 vs 3590 (14%).
- **Likely trigger** (a fix lead, not fixed here): fine's periodic faces carry a sub-micron seam offset (torus `V=[-3.5e-6, 2π-3.5e-6]` vs medium's exact `[0,2π]`), invisible to BRepCheck but apparently enough to tip OCCT's export into the degenerate case. The fix probably belongs in stage-3 pcurve/seam construction (snap periodic domains to `[0,2π]`, or attach exact-circle pcurves for circular edges so OCCT doesn't resample).

## Can it be recognized before shipping a bad STEP?

Not by any in-memory check — the in-memory solid is pristine by every geometric *and* topological measure, so BRepCheck/volume/UV-bounds all pass. **The only reliable detector is a round-trip.** So I added an unconditional **STEP round-trip self-check** in [stage4.rs](src/stage4.rs): measure the in-memory solid volume before the write, re-read the written STEP once, and warn if the re-imported volume differs by >1%. It:
- Cleanly separates good from bad (medium 3e-5 → silent OK; fine 0.14 → a prominent `Stage 4.1 WARNING` naming the likely pcurve degeneracy).
- Has **no false positives** across the corpus (all passing models ≤2.1e-3; freeform B-splines round-trip at ~1e-14).
- Requires **no reference STEP** (works on any output), and the `--compare` path now reuses the same single re-read, with its error behavior unchanged.

## State
- Full diagnosis + fix lead recorded in [TODO.md](TODO.md).
- Throwaway probe removed; temporary debug scaffolding removed.
- Clippy clean (with and without `--tests`); stage3 99/7, stage4 28/8, competition 3/3, lib 90 — all unchanged, no regressions.
- Folded into the step-5-debug commit `31da32a` (title updated to mention the self-check). Working tree clean.

One note on scope: you asked to detect it "before the STEP round-trip phase," but the investigation showed the bug *is* the round-trip and nothing in-memory reveals it — so the self-check detects it at output time (before brepper returns), which serves the underlying goal of not silently shipping a STEP that misrepresents the computed solid. If you'd prefer this gated behind a flag (it does add one STEP re-read per run, which matters for very large models like 3DBenchy), that's a quick change.

/// Re-read the just-written STEP and verify it. The **round-trip self-check**
/// always runs: OCCT re-expresses every pcurve as a B-spline on STEP export,
/// and on some meshes a periodic-face pcurve degenerates (observed on
/// `pipe_elbow_10_fine`: an end-cap plane's boundary collapsed to a point),
/// producing a re-import that is still topologically valid and closed but
/// bounds a different volume. The in-memory solid passes every geometric and
/// topological BRepCheck, so **only a round-trip catches this** — hence we
/// compare the re-imported volume against the in-memory volume measured before
/// the write. When `--compare` is set, the reference comparison reuses the
/// same re-read shape.
fn verify_output(
    output_path: &str,
    in_memory_volume: f64,
    config: &Config,
) -> Result<(), Stage4Error> {
    let comparing = config.compare_shape.is_some();

    let output_shape = match crate::read_step_file(output_path) {
        Ok(shape) => shape,
        Err(e) => {
            // With --compare an unreadable output is a hard error (the round
            // trip could not be validated); without it (e.g. -o /dev/null)
            // there is simply nothing to self-check.
            if comparing {
                eprintln!("  Compare 4.1: ERROR failed to re-read written STEP file {output_path}: {e}");
                return Err(Stage4Error::Compare(Stage4CompareError::RoundTrip {
                    path: output_path.to_string(),
                    error: e,
                }));
            }
            return Ok(());
        }
    };

    let output_vol = compute_volume(&output_shape);

    // Round-trip self-check (no reference needed).
    if in_memory_volume > 0.0 {
        let self_diff = (output_vol - in_memory_volume).abs() / in_memory_volume;
        if self_diff > STEP_ROUNDTRIP_VOLUME_TOL {
            eprintln!(
                "  Stage 4.1 WARNING: written STEP volume {output_vol:.6} mm\u{00b3} differs from \
                 the in-memory solid ({in_memory_volume:.6} mm\u{00b3}) by {self_diff:.2e} — the \
                 STEP file does not faithfully represent the computed geometry (a periodic-face \
                 pcurve likely degenerated on export). The in-memory solid is valid; the written \
                 file is not."
            );
        } else if !config.quiet {
            eprintln!("  Stage 4.1: STEP round-trip volume check OK (rel diff {self_diff:.2e})");
        }
    }

    // Reference comparison (--compare).
    if comparing {
        compare_output_to_step(&output_shape, output_vol, config)?;
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

**After:**
```
/// Re-read the just-written STEP and verify it against the reference
/// (`--compare` only). One re-read feeds two checks:
///
///  1. **Round-trip self-check** — the re-imported volume vs the in-memory
///     solid volume measured before the write. OCCT re-expresses every pcurve
///     as a B-spline on STEP export, and on some meshes a periodic-face pcurve
///     degenerates (observed on `pipe_elbow_10_fine`: an end-cap plane's
///     boundary collapsed to a point), producing a re-import that is still
///     topologically valid and closed but bounds a different volume. The
///     in-memory solid passes every geometric and topological BRepCheck, so
///     **only a round-trip catches this** — the reference comparison alone
///     cannot tell "the geometry was wrong" from "a correct solid was
///     corrupted on export", which is what this self-check disambiguates.
///  2. **Reference comparison** — volume and distance vs the `--compare` STEP.
fn verify_output(
    output_path: &str,
    in_memory_volume: f64,
    config: &Config,
) -> Result<(), Stage4Error> {
    // An unreadable output (including -o /dev/null) means the round trip could
    // not be validated, which fails --compare.
    let output_shape = match crate::read_step_file(output_path) {
        Ok(shape) => shape,
        Err(e) => {
            eprintln!("  Compare 4.1: ERROR failed to re-read written STEP file {output_path}: {e}");
            return Err(Stage4Error::Compare(Stage4CompareError::RoundTrip {
                path: output_path.to_string(),
                error: e,
            }));
        }
    };

    let output_vol = compute_volume(&output_shape);

    // 1. Round-trip self-check (against the in-memory solid, no reference).
    if in_memory_volume > 0.0 {
        let self_diff = (output_vol - in_memory_volume).abs() / in_memory_volume;
        if self_diff > STEP_ROUNDTRIP_VOLUME_TOL {
            eprintln!(
                "  Stage 4.1 WARNING: written STEP volume {output_vol:.6} mm\u{00b3} differs from \
                 the in-memory solid ({in_memory_volume:.6} mm\u{00b3}) by {self_diff:.2e} — the \
                 STEP file does not faithfully represent the computed geometry (a periodic-face \
                 pcurve likely degenerated on export). The in-memory solid is valid; the written \
                 file is not."
            );
        } else if !config.quiet {
            eprintln!("  Stage 4.1: STEP round-trip volume check OK (rel diff {self_diff:.2e})");
        }
    }

    // 2. Reference comparison.
    compare_output_to_step(&output_shape, output_vol, config)?;
    Ok(())
}
```
</details>

Based on the web search results, I found relevant information about OCCT's handling of STEP export with toroidal surfaces and related issues:

## Key Findings

**Degenerate Toroidal Surface Issue:**
In STEP export, an inner void can get transformed into a degenerate toroid, causing the void to only cut out a ~0.7mm thick shell of a sphere rather than cutting out its entire volume.

**Pcurve Handling:**
OCCT controls pcurve export through write.surfacecurve.mode, and OCCT converts all trimmed_curve entities to Geom2d_BSplineCurve during import. Pcurves from a STEP file are translated if present and read.surfacecurve.mode is 0; for each edge method ShapeFix_Edge::FixSameParameter() is called, and if the resulting tolerance is greater than the maximum value between 1.0 and 2*Value of basis precision, then the pcurve is recomputed.

**Periodic Surface Handling:**
OCCT's shape healing can split toroidal, cylindrical, conical, and spherical surfaces so that each resulting segment covers not more than a defined number of degrees.

The search results indicate these are known issues in OCCT's STEP translator related to how degenerate and periodic surfaces are exported and imported, though the specific round-trip bug you're referencing may require additional technical documentation or issue tracker details.

## STEP Import/Export

**Concurrent Operations:**
"STEP read and write are safe under the contract of one reader or writer per thread (default parameter set)."

Type recognition now uses \`std::string_view\`, and duplicate-entity removal reduces file size by approximately 20% on average.

"STEP General Attributes are exported as \`property_definition\` entities" and scaling transformations are applied during export.

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdFidtkFgpiUr8kYSTUir -->

Done. Answering your questions directly:

## Was it gated behind `--compare`?

**No — I'd made it unconditional** (it re-read the output STEP on every run). You're right that gating it behind `--compare` is the correct call, so I changed it: the self-check now runs only under `--compare`, sharing the single re-read that `--compare` already does. A normal run pays **zero** extra STEP I/O. Verified: `pipe_elbow_10_fine` without `--compare` produces no re-read/warning; with `--compare` it fires the `Stage 4.1 WARNING` and then the reference check. stage4 suite still 28/8, clippy clean.

## OCCT version

brepper bundles **OCCT 7.9.3** (via `opencascade-rs`'s `occt-sys`, pinned to the `V7_9_3` tag). The current upstream release is now **8.0.0** (May 2026) / 8.0.0_p1 (June 2026) — so brepper is one major version behind.

## Is it a known OCCT bug, and is it fixed?

**Yes, this looks like a known OCCT bug with a matching fix already released in 8.0.0** — which brepper doesn't have yet. The OCCT 8.0.0 release notes list a fix that maps almost exactly onto our reproduction:

- **"partial torus creation handles inverted V range"** — our case is precisely a *partial* torus (the 90° bend) whose fine-mesh V-range came out slightly negative (`V=[-3.5e-6, 2π-3.5e-6]`), i.e. an inverted/offset V range.
- Plus **"periodic BSpline curve bounding works with the corrected tolerance"** and **"unstable PCurve processing… addressed during shape healing"** — both in the pcurve area where our end-cap pcurve degenerated.

The broader class (STEP export turning geometry into a degenerate toroid → volume error on re-import) also shows up in OCCT forum reports, so this isn't unique to us.

I recorded two concrete next steps in the TODO note (and commit `867f28a`):
1. **Bump the bundled OCCT to 8.0.0** and recheck — may fix it with no brepper change (not yet confirmed against this exact repro, so verify).
2. **Cheaper workaround to try first, no OCCT bump:** export without pcurves (`write.surfacecurve.mode=0`, `read.surfacecurve.mode=3`) and let the reader rebuild 2D pcurves from our *exact* 3D curves — sidestepping the bad B-spline pcurve conversion entirely. Needs a binding to `Interface_Static::SetIVal` before the STEP write.

Working tree is clean; everything folded into the amended step-5-debug commit.

Sources:
- [OCCT Releases](https://github.com/Open-Cascade-SAS/OCCT/releases)
- [OCCT 8.0.0 release notes](https://github.com/Open-Cascade-SAS/OCCT/releases/tag/V8_0_0)
- [OCCT STEP wiki (surfacecurve modes / pcurve recompute)](https://github.com/Open-Cascade-SAS/OCCT/wiki/step)
- [OCCT forum: degenerate toroidal surface in STEP files](https://dev.opencascade.org/content/degeneratetoroidalsurface-step-files-can-get-wrong-orientation)


## 👤 User

Should I upgrade to 8.0.0 (May 2026) or 8.0.0_p1 (June 2026)?


## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdFip6AsJp5VAa9V54wnc -->

Short answer: **8.0.0_p1.**

A `_p1` patch release in OCCT is a hotfix on top of the same API/ABI as 8.0.0 — no migration cost over 8.0.0, just additional stabilization fixes. The release description for it was explicitly *"a hot patch on top of 8.0.0"* consolidating fixes (Gordon surface rework, BRepGraph stabilization, thread-safe numeric parsing, modeling crash/hang fixes), with the C++17 baseline and API unchanged from 8.0.0.

So there's no reason to pin the plain 8.0.0 tag when a strictly-newer, API-compatible patch of the same release exists. The only situations where you'd prefer plain `V8_0_0`:

- **Reproducibility of an existing pin** — not your case; you're moving off 7.9.3.
- **A regression introduced by the patch** — none reported that I saw, but worth a quick corpus run after the bump either way (you'd do that regardless of which tag).

One caveat on the whole plan: the upgrade is a change to the vendored OCCT in the sibling `opencascade-rs` repo (`crates/occt-sys/OCCT` is pinned to the `V7_9_3` tag), and its FFI bindings (`opencascade-sys`) are generated against that source. A 7.9.3 → 8.0.0 jump crosses a **major** version, so expect some binding/API churn (renamed/removed symbols, signature changes) — this is more involved than repointing a submodule, which is why I'd still **try the `write.surfacecurve.mode=0` workaround first**: it's a one-line `Interface_Static::SetIVal` in brepper and confirms whether the pcurve-export path is really the culprit before you take on a major OCCT bump.

If you do bump, go straight to `V8_0_0_p1`.

12	/// Read a STEP file and return the loaded shape.
13	///
14	/// Uses \`step_control::StepReader\` which holds the global STEP I/O mutex

16	/// STEP reader state.
17	pub fn read_step_file(path: &str) -> Result<OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Shape>, String> {
18	    let mut reader = step_control::StepReader::new();
19	    let status = reader.read_file_charptr(path);

22	    }

24	    reader.transfer_roots(&progress);

33	pub fn write_step_file(shape: &topo_ds::Shape, path: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
34	    interface::Static::set_c_val("write.step.schema", "AP214");
35	    interface::Static::set_c_val("write.step.product.name", "brepper");
36	    let mut writer = step_control::StepWriter::new();
37	    let progress = message::ProgressRange::new();
38	    let status = writer.transfer_shape_stepmodeltype_bool_progressrange(

40	        step_control::StepModelType::Asis,
41	        true,
42	        &progress,

45	        return Err(format!("STEPControl_Writer::Transfer returned {status:?}"));
46	    }
47	    let status = writer.write(path);
48	    if status != if_select::ReturnStatus::Retdone {
49	        return Err(format!("STEPControl_Writer::Write returned {status:?}"));
50	    }
51	    Ok(())
52	}

54	/// Suppress OCCT's default console output (transfer statistics, etc.).
55	/// Replaces the default stdout printer with one that only prints Fail-level messages.
56	pub fn suppress_occt_messages() {
57	    let mut messenger_handle = message::default_messenger();
58	    let messenger = messenger_handle.get_mut();
59	    // Remove the default PrinterOStream (which prints to stdout)
60	    let type_desc = message::PrinterOStream::get_type_descriptor();
61	    messenger.remove_printers(type_desc);
62	    // Add a printer that only shows failures
63	    let printer = message::PrinterOStream::new_gravity(message::Gravity::Fail);
64	    let printer_handle = message::PrinterOStream::to_handle(printer);
65	    let printer_base = printer_handle.to_handle_printer();
66	    messenger.add_printer(&printer_base);
67	}

pub fn write_step_file(shape: &topo_ds::Shape, path: &str) -> Result<(), String> {

pub fn write_step_file(shape: &topo_ds::Shape, path: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
    interface::Static::set_c_val("write.step.schema", "AP214");
    interface::Static::set_c_val("write.step.product.name", "brepper");
    // Do not write 2D pcurves. OCCT re-expresses every pcurve as a B-spline
    // on export, and for some meshes a periodic-face pcurve degenerates
    // (pipe_elbow_10_fine: an end-cap plane's boundary collapsed to a point,
    // 14% volume loss on re-import) — an OCCT bug (partial-torus inverted-V
    // handling, reportedly fixed in OCCT 8.0.0; brepper bundles 7.9.3).
    // Omitting pcurves makes the reader rebuild them from our exact 3D edge
    // curves, sidestepping the bad conversion. (STEP write.surfacecurve.mode.)
    interface::Static::set_i_val("write.surfacecurve.mode", 0);
```

    does NOT work — do not retry.** Set `write.surfacecurve.mode=0` (verified

    degenerates for this partial-torus periodic face. The bug is in OCCT's
    pcurve computation for the partial torus, hit on both the write and read
    paths, which is why only the OCCT upgrade (or generating the geometry so
    the periodic domain lands exactly on `[0, 2π]`) can fix it.
    `interface::Static::set_i_val(name, val)` is the available binding if a
    future attempt needs it.

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdFjHLDDpCHZLcZ1qs9if -->

Here's the outcome.

## Is `Interface_Static::SetIVal` available?

**Yes.** The safe wrapper is `opencascade_sys::interface::Static::set_i_val(name: &str, val: i32) -> bool` (wrapping `Interface_Static::SetIVal` via the `Interface_Static_set_i_val` FFI), alongside `set_c_val`/`set_r_val` and the getters `i_val`/`c_val`/`r_val`. `write_step_file` already uses `set_c_val` for the schema, so the pattern was ready to go.

## Did mode = 0 work?

**No — I tried it and it doesn't fix the issue**, so I reverted it. The evidence is actually quite illuminating:

- Setting `write.surfacecurve.mode = 0` **did** take effect — the written STEP dropped from 20 B-spline pcurves + seam/surface curves to **zero pcurves**, leaving only the (exact) 3D edge curves.
- But the re-imported volume was **still 3087 mm³** (14% low). Adding `read.surfacecurve.mode = 3` (3D-preferred) didn't change it either.

That's a meaningful result: it **relocates the bug**. The degeneracy is *not* in the writer's pcurve→B-spline conversion, as I'd hypothesized — OCCT **rebuilds the pcurve from the 3D curve on read**, and *that reconstruction also degenerates* for this partial-torus periodic face. So the bug lives in OCCT 7.9.3's pcurve computation for the partial torus, hit on **both** the write and read paths. No `surfacecurve.mode` setting can route around it.

## Bottom line

The `surfacecurve.mode` lever is a dead end; the real fix is the **OCCT 8.0.0_p1 upgrade** (whose "partial torus creation handles inverted V range" fix matches this repro directly). I've recorded the failed workaround in the TODO note so the next session doesn't retry it, and noted that `interface::Static::set_i_val` is available if some future attempt needs it.

Working tree is clean; both experimental changes reverted (`lib.rs` back to its committed state). The only lasting change this turn is the corrected TODO note, folded into the step-5-debug commit. clippy clean.

One note: I amended the commit to correct the note, since the previously-committed version suggested the mode=0 workaround as promising — leaving that in would have misled the next session. If you'd rather I hadn't amended for a docs-only correction, say so and I'll adjust.
